After all that running around and arguing I was doing earlier, I just... I dropped into this really heavy, empty feeling. I'll just sit at the kitchen table and cry over absolutely nothing. It feels like I just can't get myself back.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned being really wound up and restless earlier, can you tell me more about what that felt like?\n\nPatient: Well, at first I just felt really fired up. Like I was almost unstoppable with my craft business and getting all the house rules in order. But... I guess I was also pretty on edge. I would snap at my husband over the smallest little comment. It just felt like he was trying to hold me back, you know? So I'd just get really angry with him over basically nothing.\n\nDoctor: Did you have a decreased need for sleep during the times you felt really fired up and unstoppable?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did. For a while there, I was only sleeping maybe two or three hours a night. But I'd wake up just... completely ready to go. It honestly felt like sleep was just wasting time that I needed for my projects.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about anything when you're feeling really down?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't think I feel guilty about anything specific. It's... it's more just that heavy, empty feeling I was talking about. I don't really sit around blaming myself for things, I just feel really sad for no reason.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling really down, do you still enjoy activities that you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. When I get like that, I just stop doing everything I normally like. I haven't even touched my knitting basket or my garden beds lately. I stopped going to my book club, and I didn't even sign up to help at the church food pantry like I usually do. It just... it all feels like way too much trouble, and honestly, none of it seems worth doing when I feel that down.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing your name called without any periods where the voice stopped?\n\nPatient: I haven't heard my name called like that.
